# Architecture read of dubplate

This report merges an architecture read of the dubplate repository at commit `b5211ce`. Each scope was read against one bar:

> "It should be a system that an experienced developer would be proud of."

The read covered 49 scopes: 44 Go packages, commands, and tools, three reads of the web app (Svelte idiom, accessibility, and structure), and two repo-wide reads (naming and structure). 13 scopes were judged proud and 36 were not. The reads raised 633 findings: 95 high, 259 medium, and 279 low.

The proud scopes are `internal/atomicfile`, `internal/harness/cassette`, `internal/harness/stub`, `internal/harness/subsonic/fixture`, `internal/harness/subsonic/session`, `internal/harness/subsonic/stack`, `internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule`, `internal/id`, `internal/opensubsonic`, `internal/opensubsonic/client`, `internal/ops/admin`, `internal/store`, and `tools/close-evidence`.

The packages are careful on their own terms. Error mapping, signal handling, secret handling in doctor output, and transaction safety show real judgment. What keeps most scopes below the bar is what an agent would copy: core vocabulary coined again at each package boundary, test-only seams built into production signatures, exports with no production caller, and comments that defend a copy instead of removing it.

## How to read this report

- **Repo-wide** merges the naming read and the structure read. Each entry lists its sites, including the package reads that raised the same point.
- **Patterns** holds findings that recur in three or more packages. Patterns P1 to P4 hold the full finding for every site, and those findings are not repeated under By package. Patterns P5 to P9 list their sites, and the full finding stays in the package table, marked with the pattern number.
- **By package** gives each scope its verdict and the findings that no pattern holds, high severity first.
- **Unused exports** lists every export the reads found with no production caller, with a ruling for each.
- **Best passages** names three passages to copy.

Paths are relative to the repository root.

## Repo-wide

Two reads looked across package lines. Both judged the repository not proud.

- The naming read: Each package is carefully written on its own: the comments are precise, the sentinels are mapped, and the id parser is a good model. But the core vocabulary was coined again at each package boundary. "Who acted", "not signed in", "seam", "setting reader", "id", and "setting key" each exist in two to four forms, joined by conversion code in cmd and internal/ops/admin. An agent copying any one package would add a fifth form instead of reusing one, so the boundary findings are high severity even though each package passes the gate.
- The structure read: Locally the packages are careful. config is a true leaf, the ops/opshttp projection is clean, table ownership is tested, and composition sits in cmd. The boundaries between packages, though, have drifted in ways an agent would copy. Concepts are duplicated across packages (Actor, the member count, free-space probes, setting readers). Health checks and other packages' facts sit in cmd and store, against records 0013 and 0011. A process-wide transliterator global lives in the matcher. The harness tree is split by history rather than by job, and its on-disk case layout is hand-copied into cmd.

Where the two reads, or a package read, raised the same point, the entries below merge them and list every site. The package-level findings named in "Sites" also appear in their own package tables.

### Vocabulary at package boundaries

#### One actor, three types

Severity: high.

Three types name the principal behind a change. `ops.Actor` (`internal/ops/ops.go:24`, built by LocalActor and NetworkActor) and `auth.Actor` (`internal/auth/roster.go:156`, built by MemberActor and OperatorActor) each encode the same `local:<name>` and `member:<hex>` wire form by hand. `auth.Actor.String` at `roster.go:211` says it does this "so the two records name one actor alike", a comment that admits the duplication and keeps the two in step by hand. `settings.Author` (`internal/settings/write.go:51`) is a third, stringly typed shape that cmd fills from `auth.OperatorActor(...).String()` (`cmd/dubplate-server/settings.go:46`). Conversions happen at `internal/ops/admin/admin.go:100` (rosterActor, which drops the channel) and in cmd.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/ops.go:24` (naming and structure reads)
- `internal/settings/write.go:51` (internal/settings read)

Fix: Keep one Actor type in a leaf package that both auth and ops already import (`internal/identity` is the natural home), with its constructors and one String or MarshalText method. Have auth's roster audit and `settings.Write` take that type directly. Delete `auth.Actor`, `settings.Author`, `rosterActor`, and the mirroring comment, so the compiler enforces the agreement.

#### Four sentinels for "no valid session"

Severity: high.

There are four sentinels for "the request has no valid session": auth.ErrNotSignedIn (internal/auth/request.go:15, "auth: no valid session"), intake.ErrUnauthenticated (internal/intake/tuswrap.go:21, "intake: no valid session"), opshttp.ErrUnauthenticated (mount.go:16, "opshttp: no valid session"), and ops.ErrUnauthenticated (internal/ops/errors.go:44). cmd re-wraps one into another at cmd/dubplate-server/intake.go:77-78 and api.go:36-37. The two mounts also declare separate Verifier func types (intake.Verifier at tuswrap.go:28 and opshttp.Verifier at mount.go:24) for the same "check this request" role. An experienced reviewer would ask why errors.Is needs a translation table for one fact.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/opshttp/mount.go:16` (naming and internal/ops/opshttp reads)
- `cmd/dubplate-server/intake.go:73` (cmd/dubplate-server read)
- `internal/ops/errors.go:132` (internal/ops read)

Fix: Let the mounts recognise one sentinel. ops.ErrUnauthenticated already carries the wire code, so intake and opshttp should test for it, and auth.CheckRequest should wrap it, or auth.ErrNotSignedIn should become that sentinel. Delete the per-mount copies and the re-wrapping in cmd.

#### "Seam" names interfaces and structs

Severity: high.

"Seam" names both interfaces and concrete structs. The interfaces are `auth.Seam` (`internal/auth/seam.go:21`), `ops.AdminSeam` (`internal/ops/admin.go:126`), and `ops.SubmissionSeam` (`internal/ops/submissions.go:142`). The structs are `intake.Seam` (`internal/intake/seam.go:43`) and `admin.Seam` (`internal/ops/admin/admin.go:62`), and the field `auth.AdminConfig.Seam` (`internal/auth/admin.go:15`) uses the word too. "Seam" is a testing-architecture word, not a role: a reader of `cmd/dubplate-server/api.go:22` learns nothing about what the value does. `auth.Seam` is also the Java habit of declaring an interface beside its only implementation, MagicLinkSeam, and its Read method is reached through the interface only by `seam_test.go:299`. The ops doc says there are "two seams", but the jobs vocabulary in `ops.go` has none.

Sites:

- `internal/auth/seam.go:21` (naming and structure reads)
- `internal/auth/seam.go:38` (internal/auth read)
- `internal/ops/admin.go:126` (internal/ops read)
- `internal/ops/admin/admin.go:64` (internal/ops/admin read)
- `internal/ops/opshttp/authorize.go:29` (internal/ops/opshttp read)

Fix: Return the concrete `*MagicLinkSeam` and let a consumer that needs to substitute it declare the narrow interface it uses. Name the interfaces by behavior (`auth.Verifier`, `ops.Admin`, `ops.Submissions`) and the implementations for what they are (for example `admin.Service` and `intake.Service`). Drop Read from the method set until a production caller needs it, fix the ops doc's count, and keep "seam" for prose in decision records.

#### Setting readers in four shapes, homed in auth

Severity: high.

Reading one typed setting takes at least four shapes. The first is `auth.Setting[T]` (`internal/auth/handlers.go:75`) with its constructors DurationSetting, BodyCap, GenericAnswer, and ReadLimits in `auth/config.go`. The second is bare functions taking `(ctx, *settings.Accessor)`, such as `intake.SweepInterval` (`internal/intake/config.go:424`) and `intake.CheckWorkers` (`:431`). The third is closure factories typed `func(context.Context) (T, error)` without the named type (intake LimitsFrom, WindowsFrom, FreeSpaceFloor). The fourth is per-package helpers (`local.JobRetention`, `admin.ReauthWindow`, `backup.Readers`). Because the general helper lives in auth, ops/admin imports auth just to call `auth.DurationSetting` (`internal/ops/admin/config.go:77`), and intake, which must not import auth, re-implements it as readError (`internal/intake/config.go:440`), whose comment says it spells out "the key-first form auth prints".

Sites:

- `internal/auth/handlers.go:75` (naming and structure reads)
- `internal/intake/config.go:326` (internal/intake read)
- `internal/ops/admin/admin.go:50` (internal/ops/admin read)
- `internal/ops/local/local.go:43` (internal/ops/local read)
- `internal/ops/local/config.go:36` (internal/ops/local read)

Fix: Move `Setting[T]`, SettingError, and the Duration, Int, and Bool reader constructors into `internal/settings`, below every consumer, as one type (for example `settings.Reader[T]`) with one error wrap. auth, intake, ops/admin, and ops/local then use that type, and the intake copy goes away. Name each reader for the value it returns: `CheckWorkers` becomes `CheckWorkerCount`.

#### Ids outside `internal/id` (record 0012)

Severity: high.

Record 0012 says an id is the internal/id type or a named type built on it, and only id.Parse reads one. intake carries ids as raw []byte through submission.go, seam.go, cleanup.go, receipt.go, sweep.go, and startup.go, parses them in its own parseID (submission.go:112), and formats them in wireID (seam.go:350). ops.Handle is a string type (internal/ops/ops.go:99), and ParseHandle (:104) calls id.Parse only to discard the value. ops.Member.UserID is a string (internal/ops/admin.go:9). internal/ops/admin/members.go:87 spells hex.EncodeToString(m.ID[:]) instead of calling the id's own formatter, and admin.parseUserID (internal/ops/admin/admin.go:106) and local's handleOf/parse in internal/ops/local/jobs.go repeat the hex round trip. On the wire, JobStatus calls its id "id" (ops.go:136) while submissions call theirs "submission_id".

Sites:

- `internal/intake/submission.go:112` (naming read)
- `internal/id/id.go:47` (internal/id read)
- `internal/identity/identity.go:202` (internal/id and internal/identity reads)
- `internal/intake/seam.go:350` (internal/intake read)
- `internal/intake/worker.go:73` (internal/intake read)
- `internal/ops/ops.go:104` (internal/ops read)
- `internal/ops/local/jobs.go:99` (internal/ops/local read)

Fix: Use id.ID, or named types on it like identity.UserID and a SubmissionID, everywhere in process. Give id.ID MarshalText and UnmarshalText so wire structs carry the typed id and JSON does the hex. Delete parseID, wireID, parseUserID, and the hex.EncodeToString calls. Name the wire field for its kind (job_id) to match submission_id and user_id.

#### A setting is "name" in one message and "key" in the next

Severity: high.

One setting's identifier is "name" in one message and "key" in the next. SettingEntry.Name is tagged `json:"name"` (admin.go:67), while SetSettingRequest, SetSettingResponse, ResetSettingRequest, and ResetSettingResponse use Key `json:"key"` (:85, :92, :99, :105). The TS page copies the split: web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte declares Entry.name and Written.key, then joins them with `e.name === key` (around :140). The config package splits the same way: KeyDecl.Name (internal/config/keys.go:43) and WallRow.Name (:60), but Setting.Key (internal/config/settings.go:60), Threshold.Key (thresholds.go:88), KeyError.Key, and store.SettingsRow.Key. A client author has to learn that two field names mean one thing, and an agent adding a field will pick whichever it saw last.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/admin.go:67` (naming and internal/ops reads)

Fix: "key" wins. The store, the write and reset messages, settings.Outcome, and the environment all call it Key, and 0006 uses "setting key". Rename SettingEntry.Name, KeyDecl.Name, and WallRow.Name to Key, update openapi.yaml and the TS Entry type, and import that type from lib/api instead of redeclaring it in the page.

#### "Mark" for a field of type Sensitivity

Severity: medium.

A setting's sensitivity class is called "mark". Its type is config.Sensitivity, yet the field is KeyDecl.Mark, WallRow.Mark, and Threshold.Mark, the wire field is `mark` (admin.go:71, mapped by markNames in internal/ops/admin/settings.go:31), and the TS map is SETTING_MARKS (web/src/lib/copy/deck.ts:249). A field named differently from its own type is a smell. "Mark" also already means the brand mark in the web app (Mark.svelte).

Sites:

- `internal/ops/admin.go:71` (naming read)
- `internal/ops/admin/settings.go:16` (internal/ops/admin read)

Fix: Rename the field Sensitivity in Go and sensitivity on the wire and in TS, so it matches its type.

#### Wire enums in kebab-case and snake_case

Severity: medium.

Wire enum spelling mixes kebab-case and snake_case, sometimes inside one object. One SettingEntry carries source "env-seed" (evaluate.go:20; openapi.yaml:426) beside apply "next_operation" and mark "not_sensitive". One error body's `code` is snake for ops.Code (internal/ops/errors.go:17-30, e.g. "invalid_request") but kebab for reject.Code (internal/intake/reject/reject.go:19-47, e.g. "bad-extension"), and opshttp writes both. A client that switches on code values has to handle both spellings.

Sites:

- `internal/settings/evaluate.go:20` (naming read)

Fix: Choose snake_case, which most of the ops contract already uses, for every wire enum. Since the kebab spellings are fixed in docs/specs/2026-09-11-configuration-homes-design.md and the copy-final spec, make the change as one contract revision with the specs, openapi.yaml, and the TS outcome tables.

#### Reason, verdict, and refusal for one value

Severity: medium.

Three fields hold the same reject.Reason under three names: SubmissionEvent.Reject (:87), SubmissionFile.Verdict (:97), and SubmissionWarning.Reason (:103). The gate's own types add more: gate.Refusal, gate.ZIPRefusal, gate.FileReason, and FileResult.Verdict / AlbumResult.Verdict of type reject.Code. On the ops side there are ops.FileReject and ops.Rejects (errors.go:65, :73), built from gate.Refusal at internal/intake/seam.go:107-117. The TS side says 'refused' and API_REFUSALS. Record 0008 makes the verdict a unit-level decision, so "verdict" holding a per-file reason also blurs that rule.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/submissions.go:87` (naming read)

Fix: Choose one noun per level. Use Reason for a reject.Reason field everywhere (SubmissionEvent.Reason, SubmissionFile.Reason). Keep Verdict for the album-level pass or refuse that 0008 defines. Rename gate.Refusal and ops.Rejects to one shared name (Rejects, matching the package reject), and make FileReason and FileReject one type.

#### ClientIP and ClientAddress

Severity: low.

The request's client address is ClientIP in serve (serve.ClientIP), in auth (AdminConfig.ClientIP), and in every log key ("client_ip", e.g. internal/auth/request.go:48). In ops it is ClientAddress() (ops.go:88), with the parameter clientAddr (ops.go:54), and the file defining serve.ClientIP is serve/clientaddr.go.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/ops.go:88` (naming read)

Fix: ClientIP wins, since it is the majority and the log key. Rename Actor.ClientAddress to ClientIP and clientaddr.go to clientip.go.

#### Two Violation vocabularies for one protocol

Severity: medium.

Two packages validate a Subsonic response with two vocabularies. conformance.Checker reports Violation{Pointer, Reason}, while xmlrule.Validator (internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule) reports Violation{Ordinal, Attr, Message, Source}. Both expose Check, Outcome, Valid, and Result with different meanings, and the trees are named opensubsonic and harness/subsonic for one protocol. A reader cannot tell whether a Violation's text is in Reason or Message without checking which package it came from.

Sites:

- `internal/opensubsonic/conformance:1` (naming read)
- `internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule/check.go:16` (internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule read)

Fix: Give both one Violation shape: a location (JSON pointer, or XPath for XML) and a Message. Name both the Checker type and a Check method. Put the protocol under one tree name, subsonic, with opensubsonic only where the OpenSubsonic extensions are meant.

#### "Store" in two senses

Severity: medium.

"store" means two things. auth.Store is the auth file directory (auth.Open, StoreDir, OpenEpochStore, OpenNonceStore with a parameter named store that shadows the store package). store is the SQLite database package. cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go prints an "auth store" row from auth.StoreRowStatus (doctor.go:105, :211) beside a "store" row from store.DoctorRow (:60, :100). An operator reading doctor output, and a developer reading auth, both have to work out which store is meant.

Sites:

- `internal/auth/doctor.go:42` (naming read)

Fix: Keep "store" for the database package. Rename auth.Store to auth.Dir or auth.Keyring, depending on what it holds, and rename the doctor row to match.

#### Resolve, Check, and Validate with several meanings

Severity: medium.

The verbs "Resolve" and "Check" each mean several things. serve.ResolveConfig parses environment values, serve.ResolveTrustedProxy builds a value, and intake.ResolveStaging only validates. serve.ValidatePort (config.go:55) and serve.CheckProxyHeader (clientaddr.go:54) sit in one package doing the same job under two verbs. Across packages, gate.CheckFile and CheckAlbum return verdict results, auth.CheckAdmin authorizes, auth.CheckRequest verifies and refreshes a session, EpochStore.CheckSession and CheckLink return a refusal error, and intake.CheckWorkers is a setting read.

Sites:

- `internal/serve/config.go:55` (naming read)
- `internal/intake/config.go:401` (internal/intake read)

Fix: Give each verb one meaning: Parse for string to value, Validate for value to error, Check for a probe returning a result, and Verify for credential checks (auth.VerifyRequest). Rename ResolveStaging to ValidateStaging, CheckProxyHeader to ValidateProxyHeader, and CheckWorkers to CheckWorkerCount, following the reader rule above.

#### Two address normalizers

Severity: medium.

There are two address normalizers. config.ParseAdminAddress (config.go:260) re-implements identity.parseLower (internal/identity/identity.go:147) but skips identity.NormalizeAddress's length and invisible-rune checks, even though its comment says no two readers can differ. Sentinels repeat the split: auth.ErrInvalidMemberAddress (internal/auth/roster.go, "address does not parse as a login address") sits beside identity.ErrInvalidAddress (identity.go:32). The setting is called AdminEmail while every type says Address.

Sites:

- `internal/config/config.go:260` (naming read)
- `internal/auth/roster.go:239` (internal/auth read)

Fix: config should call identity.NormalizeAddress and wrap identity.ErrInvalidAddress. Delete the copy and auth's separate sentinel, or make auth's sentinel wrap identity's. Spell the setting in the Address vocabulary, as 0006 requires.

#### Package name `local`

Severity: medium.

The package name local says where the code runs, not what it holds. It holds the job runner: local.New returns *Runner, alongside local.Config, local.JobRetention, and local.BackupConfigFrom (call sites cmd/dubplate-server/backup.go:99-102). At call sites local.New and local.Config read as nothing.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/local:1` (naming read)

Fix: Rename the package jobs (jobs.New, jobs.Runner, jobs.Config), or runner. Move BackupConfigFrom next to the backup job it configures.

#### TS copy constants named for a narrower scope

Severity: medium.

TS copy constants are named for a narrower idea than their use. AUTH_OUTCOMES.failed is the generic API-failure line for every v1 call (v1.ts:77, :89, :102). ROSTER_RETRY (web/src/lib/copy/deck.ts:123) is the retry label of the generic AdminPage.svelte (:20, :56) and WriteNotice.svelte (:36), including on server-settings, which has nothing to do with the roster. The next page author will either reuse a misleading name or add a duplicate.

Sites:

- `web/src/lib/api/v1.ts:77` (naming read)

Fix: Move them under their real scope, for example API_OUTCOMES.failed and ADMIN_RETRY, so lib/api imports nothing named for auth.

#### Members, roster, and users

Severity: low.

The group's people are "members" in 0006, but the code also says "roster" and "users": auth.Roster, CLI help "Manage the roster of group members" (member.go:42, also :109, :150, :197), ROSTER_EMPTY_STATE (deck.ts:300), loadRoster and roster-outcome ids on the members page, identity.UserID, the user_id wire fields, and the users table, whose foreign keys are named owner, uploader, and user_id (internal/store/migrations/0003, 0005, 0006).

Sites:

- `cmd/dubplate-server/member.go:42` (naming read)

Fix: Keep "member" for people and "roster" only for the auth package's list type, if at all. CLI help should say "Manage the group's members". Leave the users table and UserID as storage names, but name foreign keys consistently (member_id, or user_id throughout, not owner and uploader for the same reference).

#### Two sentinel-text conventions

Severity: low.

Sentinel text follows two conventions. auth, tagio, xmlrule, identity, intake (tuswrap), notify, opshttp, and stack prefix their messages with "pkg: ". ops, store, settings, serve, gate, matcher, musicbrainz, config, and webapp do not (for example internal/store/conn.go:36 "store busy", internal/ops/errors.go:36 "unavailable"). The Go style guides advise against package-name prefixes, since the caller's wrap supplies context. Mixing both styles makes logs read unevenly and gives new code no rule to follow.

Sites:

- `internal/auth/request.go:15` (naming read)

Fix: Drop the "pkg: " prefixes so every sentinel is a bare lowercase phrase, and let the %w wraps supply the context. Add the rule to the conventions doc.

Note: Pattern P7 lists the package-level sites.

#### Job types shaped like wire operations (record 0003)

Severity: low.

JobStatus, JobStatusRequest, and JobStatusResponse (ops.go:135-170) and the related job request types carry JSON tags and Request/Response names as if they were wire operations. openapi.yaml has no start_backup, job_status, or cancel_job operation, and there is no Op const for them. Their only callers are in process: internal/ops/local and cmd/dubplate-server/backup.go:117, :182, :203. The tags promise a contract that does not exist.

Sites:

- `internal/ops/ops.go:135` (naming read)

Fix: Remove the JSON tags and the Request/Response wrappers until an HTTP operation and its openapi entry land. Otherwise add that operation in the same change.

#### Analysis loaders named two ways

Severity: low.

Loaders in the analysis harness name their results two ways: LoadAdjudications and LoadOracleBugs return the noun, while the Load*Ledger functions name the container.

Sites:

- `internal/harness/analysis:1` (naming read)

Fix: Choose one form, either Load<Noun>s or Load<Noun>Ledger, and rename the others.

### Boundaries and placement

#### A process-wide transliterator global in the matcher

Severity: high.

matcher holds a process-wide `var transliterator atomic.Pointer[func(rune) (string, bool)]` (line 20). An exported SetTransliterator installs it and panics on nil. Its only callers are three TestMain files: matcher/testmain_test.go:19, harness/conform/testmain_test.go:20 and engine/tag/testmain_test.go:20. No production code sets it. This is global mutable registration plus an export with no production caller (record 0003). The package that supplies the function, unidecode, sits under matcher/ even though matcher never imports it, and unidecode's real job (install, fetch over net/http, extract, a doctor row) belongs to the server, not the engine. The tree claims a parent/child relationship the import graph does not have.

Sites:

- `internal/engine/matcher/translit.go:13` (structure and internal/engine/matcher reads)
- `internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/pins.go:30` (internal/engine/matcher/unidecode read)

Fix: Pass the transliterator in as a dependency. Give the matcher entry point a field such as `Translit func(rune) (string, bool)` on its config struct (or an Options value) and have callers supply it. Delete SetTransliterator and the package global. Move unidecode out of internal/engine/matcher/ to internal/unidecode, since it is a server-side asset manager the server and cmd/harness import.

#### auth imports serve for a port check

Severity: high.

auth imports serve (config.go:15) so that validateMagicLinkSource can call serve.ValidatePort(environ). The credentials package depends on the HTTP shell's port check. The arrow runs the wrong way: serve is the outer shell that mounts auth's handlers, so the leaf now knows the shell. The comment also cites 'spec:1289' instead of stating the rule.

Sites:

- `internal/auth/config.go:378` (structure read)

Fix: Have the composition root resolve the port once through serve.ResolveConfig and pass the validated value, or the built base URL, into auth's config as a plain value. Then delete the auth→serve import. State the invariant in words, not as a spec line number.

#### auth holds both credentials and the member roster

Severity: high.

auth does two jobs. It handles credentials: magic links, sessions and CheckRequest. It also handles the membership roster: Roster, Member, CheckAdmin, EarliestAdmin, and LiveMembers, which runs `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE tombstoned_at IS NULL`, plus ownership of the users and member_preferences tables (store/owners_test.go:34-35). So backup imports the whole credentials package just to count members (internal/backup/reconcile.go:59). store counts the same rows again in countMembers (internal/store/inspect.go:413).

Sites:

- `internal/auth/reads.go:38` (structure read)

Fix: Split the roster into an internal/member package that owns the users and member_preferences tables and exposes Live(ctx) and the roster reads. auth keeps credentials and imports member. backup and store's inspection call member, so the duplicate count disappears.

#### store reports on tables other packages own (records 0011 and 0013)

Severity: high.

store.Facts reports on tables other packages own: jobs (ops/local), users (auth), collections (intake) and settings rows (the SettingsRow type at :224). It hard-codes their schema versions at :159-168. The storage layer thereby knows every domain's schema, which defeats record 0011's table ownership. The doctor checks built on top of those facts (backupFactsRows :139, lastSuccessStatus, freeSpaceStatus, localCopiesStatus, formatBytes and settingsDoctorRows :24) sit in cmd/dubplate-server/doctorrows.go. Record 0013 says a check lives in the doctor.go of the package that knows the fact.

Sites:

- `internal/store/inspect.go:173` (structure and internal/store reads)
- `cmd/dubplate-server/doctorrows.go:1` (cmd/dubplate-server read)
- `internal/backup/backup.go:18` (internal/backup read)

Fix: Have each owning package report its own facts from its doctor.go: ops/local for jobs, member for users, intake for collections, settings for settings rows. store keeps only facts about the database itself (file, schema, filesystem). Move the doctorrows.go checks into backup's and settings' doctor.go.

#### Doctor rows in nine shapes, with no shared contract

Severity: high.

Record 0013 puts each doctor check in the doctor.go of the package that knows the fact, but the functions share neither a name nor a shape. Most packages export an `XRowStatus` that returns an anonymous `(status string, failed bool)` pair (`intake/doctor.go:26`, `webapp/doctor.go:18`, `auth/doctor.go:42`, `serve/doctor.go:44`, `unidecode/doctor.go:34`, `backup/doctor.go:39`), with arguments that differ per package: `(cfg, opts)`, `(ctx, environ, opts)`, `(dir)`, `(ctx, environ, reg, cfg)`, `(cfg, resolve)`, `(cfg, roots)`. `auth.AdminEmailRowStatus` returns only a string, and `store.DoctorRow` (`internal/store/doctor.go:26`) returns four values. `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:30-157` hand-adapts every one, and the row names are constants in cmd (`doctor.go:180-214`), not with the checks. A new check has no contract to follow, so an agent will invent another shape.

Sites:

- `internal/store/doctor.go:26` (naming read)
- `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:30` (structure read)
- `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:185` (cmd/dubplate-server read)
- `internal/backup/doctor.go:28` (internal/backup read)
- `internal/serve/doctor.go:198` (internal/serve read)
- `internal/serve/doctor.go:80` (internal/serve read)
- `internal/store/doctor.go:53` (internal/store read)
- `internal/webapp/doctor.go:18` (internal/webapp read)

Fix: Define one named row type in a small leaf package, for example `doctor.Row{Name, Status string; Failed bool}`. Have each package export `DoctorRows(ctx context.Context, deps Deps) []doctor.Row`, built from its already-resolved config, and own its row names. cmd's doctor then ranges over the rows instead of wiring each one by hand.

#### cmd/harness hand-copies the harness case layout

Severity: high.

`const missFileName = "misses.json"` carries the comment 'matches internal/harness/oracle's own constant' (oracle.go:35-40). That is a hand-synchronized copy of another package's on-disk format. The case layout, responses/NNN.json, is written out again in cmd at capture.go:144,269,287 and synth.go:73,76 with plain os.WriteFile instead of atomicfile, and freeze.go:68 also knows the miss file. Meanwhile cassette's doc claims to own the on-disk I/O.

Sites:

- `cmd/harness/capture.go:42` (structure read)
- `cmd/harness/freeze.go:104` (cmd/harness read)
- `internal/harness/oracle/oracle.go:40` (internal/harness/oracle read)
- `internal/harness/stub/stub.go:34` (internal/harness/stub read)

Fix: Have cassette (or oracle) export the operations on a case directory, such as WriteResponse(dir, n, body), WriteMisses and ReadMisses, so that it alone holds the file names and does atomic writes. cmd/harness then calls those operations and never builds a path.

#### opensubsonic/conformance sits apart from its XML twin

Severity: high.

opensubsonic/conformance is the JSON tier of the OpenSubsonic response grader (kin-openapi). Its only importer is internal/harness/subsonic/grade. Its sibling, the XML tier, lives at internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule and also imports openapi3 (check.go:10, binding.go:12). Two halves of one grader sit in two trees, and the no-link test in harness/conform/arch_test.go has to name conformance as a special case so the production tree can host test-only code.

Sites:

- `internal/opensubsonic/conformance/conformance.go:1` (structure read)

Fix: Move it to internal/harness/subsonic/jsonrule beside xmlrule, so that internal/opensubsonic holds only the production client. The arch test's special case then goes away.

Note: This placement is in tension with the opensubsonic reads, which treat conformance as part of the layer meant to be published as its own module (BACKLOG #96). Both readings are kept: decide whether the grader belongs to that module before moving it.

#### Import rules enforced six ways

Severity: high.

Import-graph rules are enforced in six places built three ways. internal/opensubsonic/boundary_test.go and internal/harness/conform/arch_test.go exec `go list -deps`. internal/settings/layering_test.go and internal/webapp/layering_test.go glob with parser.ImportsOnly. internal/intake/seam_test.go:719 and internal/ops/ops_test.go:346 walk and parse files. The repo-wide invariants live inside a harness tier-0 package. No one file states the layering, so the next rule gets a fourth mechanism. golangci-lint's depguard, which states these rules declaratively, is not enabled in .golangci.yml.

Sites:

- `internal/harness/conform/arch_test.go:1` (structure read)

Fix: Express the allowed and forbidden edges as depguard rules in .golangci.yml. Where a rule needs code, collect it in one internal/archtest package (or a top-level arch_test.go) sharing one go list helper, the tested violations helper from boundary_test.go. Delete the per-package copies.

#### The free-bytes probe written twice

Severity: medium.

The free-bytes probe (statfs, then Bavail * Frsize) is written twice: here and in intake/staging.FreeBytes (internal/intake/staging/staging_linux.go:48). The two use different syscall packages (syscall and x/sys/unix), and a st_dev device lookup is also written in both places. An experienced developer keeps one platform-specific filesystem helper.

Sites:

- `internal/backup/restore.go:539` (structure read)
- `internal/intake/staging/staging_linux.go:53` (internal/intake/staging read)

Fix: Put FreeBytes and Device in one small leaf package, for example internal/fsys (staging_linux.go already has the build-tagged shape), and have backup and staging both call it.

#### cmd reads auth keys directly, and auth declares the SMTP keys

Severity: medium.

newServeShellVia reads auth's keys directly with cfg.IdentitySourceValue(auth.SessionSecretKey), and does it twice: once for NewMagicLinkSeam and again in authHandlersConfig (:363-387). newMailer (:404) builds notify's config from auth.SMTP* keys that are declared in internal/auth/config.go:39-43. auth's doc and record 0010 say config.go turns settings into plain values, yet the composition root reaches past it, and the SMTP keys are declared by a package that does not send mail.

Sites:

- `cmd/dubplate-server/serve.go:216` (structure read)

Fix: Add `auth.ConfigFrom(cfg, acc) (Config, error)` in auth/config.go so it resolves every auth value once, and pass the result to both constructors. Move the SMTP key declarations and their resolution to notify's config.go.

#### AdminConfig configures every member's session check

Severity: medium.

AdminConfig configures verification of any member's session, not admin access. Its users are CheckRequest (request.go:31), apiVerifier (cmd/dubplate-server/api.go:32) and tusVerifier (cmd/dubplate-server/intake.go:73), and the latter two serve every member. The name will lead the next reader to wire the wrong config.

Sites:

- `internal/auth/admin.go:12` (structure and internal/auth reads)

Fix: Rename it after what it verifies, for example SessionCheckConfig or VerifyConfig.

#### A flat harness command tree, and two meanings of "oracle"

Severity: medium.

The harness command tree is flat. It mixes the beets-corpus verbs (capture, freeze, synth, seed-items) with the subsonic verbs (oracle-up, record-oracle, grade). 'oracle' means beets in internal/harness/oracle but Navidrome/gonic in oracle-up and record-oracle. The package tree has the same asymmetry: the beets family sits flat under internal/harness/, the subsonic family is nested under internal/harness/subsonic/, and internal/harness/conform (2453 non-test lines) has no importer and runs only from its own tests.

Sites:

- `cmd/harness/main.go:66` (structure read)

Fix: Split by job: internal/harness/corpus/{oracle,cassette,synth,analysis,conform} and internal/harness/subsonic/{grade,xmlrule,jsonrule,...}, with a matching `harness corpus ...` / `harness subsonic ...` cobra tree. Rename the subsonic 'oracle' verbs to something like reference-up and record-reference.

#### Recorder domain logic in package main

Severity: medium.

recordoracle.go is 541 lines. Lines 225-541 hold the recorder's domain logic, its wire types and the resolution of symbolic references, inside package main, where nothing else can test or reuse it. cmd/ should parse flags and wire.

Sites:

- `cmd/harness/recordoracle.go:225` (structure read)

Fix: Move the recorder into internal/harness/subsonic/record (or beside grade) and leave the cobra command as a thin RunE that calls it.

#### repoRoot written seven times

Severity: medium.

repoRoot is written seven times: here, cmd/harness/freeze.go:104, analysis/adjudication_test.go:17, conform/case_test.go:61 (as repoRootPath), conform/component_test.go:22, harness/corpus_test.go:24 and synth/synth_derive_test.go:43.

Sites:

- `internal/harness/oracle/oracle.go:81` (structure read)
- `cmd/harness/freeze.go:104` (cmd/harness read)
- `internal/harness/corpus_test.go:24` (internal/harness read)

Fix: Export one helper from a harness leaf (for example internal/harness/testdata.RepoRoot) and call it everywhere.

#### "Rung" in operator output

Severity: medium.

Doctor prints 'disabled (arrives at rung %d)' from config.ReservedClass.Rung (internal/config/keys.go:105,119-136). Internal planning vocabulary reaches the operator's output: an adopter has no idea what rung 9 is. Record 0006 asks for plain words.

Sites:

- `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:71` (structure read)
- `internal/config/keys.go:101` (internal/config read)

Fix: Replace Rung with a plain status, for example 'not yet available'. Keep the roadmap mapping in the docs, not in the config type.

#### Two package comments for serve

Severity: low.

serve has two package comments, here and at doc.go:1. go doc concatenates them and prints 'Package serve implements...' twice, clientaddr.go's first. Package docs elsewhere sit inconsistently in doc.go, collection.go, tunables.go, finding.go, xsd.go or path.go.

Sites:

- `internal/serve/clientaddr.go:1` (structure read)

Fix: Turn clientaddr.go's package comment into a file-level comment below the package clause, and keep package docs in doc.go wherever a package has more than one file.

#### Comments that cite process artifacts

Severity: low.

The package comment cites 'rung 2, pass 2a, Task 5', and auth/config.go:378 cites 'spec:1289'. Comments point at process artifacts that will rot, instead of stating the rule.

Sites:

- `internal/harness/corpus_test.go:1` (structure read)

Fix: State the invariant in the comment itself, and leave history to commits and the specs.

Note: Pattern P4 lists every package-level site.

## Patterns

These findings recur in three or more packages. They matter most, because an agent that copies a nearby file copies the pattern with it. Each entry gives the shape of the problem, the general fix, and every site.

### P1. Test-only seams in production signatures

A production constructor or config takes a function, an interface, a writer, or a twin constructor that exists only so a test can substitute it. The one production caller always passes the same value, and the doc comment often spends more lines explaining the test than the behavior. This is the pattern agents copy most, because every new constructor beside one of these grows the same extra parameter.

Fix: Give tests the stdlib seam instead: a package-level `var` that the test swaps, a value the caller already has (pass the source, not a function that fetches it), or `context.Context` for clocks and deadlines (record 0014). Delete the twin constructor and the parameter, and let the test file carry the substitution.

53 sites in 30 scopes: 16 high, 19 medium, 18 low.

| Package | Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| cmd/dubplate-server | `cmd/dubplate-server/main.go:149` | high | Every verb constructor takes `stdout, stderr io.Writer`, then repeats SilenceUsage/SilenceErrors and calls cmd.SetErr(stderr) (check.go:79, doctor.go:152, backup.go:51, restore.go:131, member.go:140,186,217, settings.go:60, unidecode.go:29,57). Cobra already provides all of this from the root: ExecuteC checks `!cmd.SilenceErrors && !c.SilenceErrors` against the root, and getOut/getErr walk to the parent. The duplication exists so tests can build a subcommand by itself. It is also inconsistent: newVersionCmd uses cmd.OutOrStdout() (main.go:168), and newServeCmd's stdout only feeds SetOut, which nothing in serve reads (serve.go:86). | Verbs take what they really need (environ, reg) and write to cmd.OutOrStdout()/cmd.ErrOrStderr(). Set SilenceUsage/SilenceErrors once on the root. Tests drive `root := newRootCmd(); root.SetArgs(...); root.SetOut(&buf)`. |
| cmd/dubplate-server | `cmd/dubplate-server/member.go:24` | high | operatorVerbs exists, as its own doc says, 'so a test builds them with values of its choosing'. main always fills it with storeWait and user.LookupId (main.go:139). resolveActor already takes the lookup as a parameter, and tests call it directly (member_test.go:342), so LookupID is a second seam stacked on the first. The seam is also only half applied: backup.go:115 calls `resolveActor(user.LookupId)` and uses storeWait directly, so the struct is not a real boundary, just the shape the member tests wanted. Every member and settings constructor became a method to carry it. The exported fields on an unexported struct in package main are a naming smell too. | Delete operatorVerbs. Make newMemberCmd and newSettingsCmd plain functions like newBackupCmd. Use storeWait directly, and swap the uid lookup through `var lookupUser = user.LookupId`. The tests set `lookupUser` and pass a short context instead of setting WriteWait. |
| cmd/dubplate-server | `cmd/dubplate-server/serve.go:158` | high | newServeShellVia takes `mount func(*serve.Shell) registrar` and `buildFS fs.FS` only for tests. Its one production caller (serve.go:125) always passes the identity `func(s *serve.Shell) registrar { return s }` and the embedded build. The registrar interface (serve.go:147) has one production implementation, and its doc admits the second is the route-inventory test. This makes nine positional parameters, a `Via` name that means nothing, and a 29-line doc on the 6-line wrapper explaining the test. Agents will copy the 'add a func parameter and a Via twin' pattern. | Keep one constructor, newServeShell, and give tests the stdlib seam: `var mountRoutes = func(s *serve.Shell) registrar { return s }` and `var buildFS = embeddedBuild` at package level, which routes_test swaps. Better still, bundle the inputs (environ, reg, prefix, bindAddrs, cfg, logger) into a small `serveInputs` struct so the signature stops growing. |
| cmd/harness | `cmd/harness/capture.go:51` | high | The package injects dependencies three different ways. capture swaps a package var of a named func type (`type fetcher`, `var doFetch fetcher = liveFetch`), and liveFetch builds a fresh http.Client on every call (capture.go:350). The fetch verbs take a *http.Client constructor parameter that main.go:77-78 always fills the same way, while their tests skip the constructor and call runFetchX directly. record-oracle swaps `var startOracles = startStack` (recordoracle.go:102). newCaptureCmd(environ, version) (capture.go:67) is also a parameter seam: main always passes os.Environ() and the package-level version, and that `version` parameter shadows the global at main.go:56. Tests elsewhere in the package already use t.Setenv. The named type `fetcher` exists only to type one var. | Use the package-var form everywhere: `var doFetch = liveFetch` with no named type, and a single package-level client, `var httpClient = &http.Client{Timeout: 30 * time.Second}`, used by capture and both fetch verbs. Read the contact with `os.LookupEnv(contactEnvVar)` inside RunE, read `version` directly, and switch capture's tests to t.Setenv. Constructors then become `newCaptureCmd()`, `newFetchUnidecodeCmd()`, and `newFetchXSDCmd()`, like the other verbs. |
| internal/auth | `internal/auth/doctor.go:87` | high | StoreRowOptions exists only to carry Geteuid, and its one production caller always passes os.Geteuid (cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:105). The only other values come from doctor_test.go:174. A zero StoreRowOptions panics at doctor.go:129. BACKLOG #177 records that the same options-struct-for-a-test shape has already spread to unidecode.Install, so agents are copying it. | Use the stdlib seam form: `var geteuid = os.Geteuid` at package level, which doctor_test swaps, and `func StoreRowStatus(cfg *config.Config) (status string, failed bool)`. Delete StoreRowOptions. |
| internal/auth | `internal/auth/epoch.go:209` | high | Subscribe, the subscribers slice (epoch.go:70), notifySubscriber with its goroutine, recover and 5s deadline (epoch.go:117-136), and the slices.Clone in advance (epoch.go:166) all serve a subscriber list that nothing subscribes to. Only epoch_test.go calls Subscribe. Every Bump pays for this path, and only a test drives it. It is a forward hook built before its consumer, which record 0003 forbids. The Bump doc spends two paragraphs (epoch.go:90-100) justifying it. | Delete Subscribe, subscribers, notifySubscriber and subscriberDeadline, and have advance return only an error. The mutex then guards only the read-then-write. The playback milestone's revoke work (BACKLOG #47) adds a notification, if it needs one, together with its subscriber. |
| internal/engine/matcher | `internal/engine/matcher/penalty.go:231` | high | AddEquality and AddPriority (penalty.go:283) take `value any, options ...any` and type-switch on PatternOption inside optionMatches (penalty.go:366). Every caller passes a string value: album.go:98, :102, :193 and :256, and even the harness (conform/component.go:321, :337 decode `var value string`). Only the options mix literals and patterns, and only in the harness. compileMediaPatterns and compileCountryPatterns (album.go:110, :204) return `[]any` as a result. This is an exported, dynamically typed API that exists to mirror Python's duck typing, and agents will copy `[]any` plumbing. | Type the two production shapes: `func (d *Distance) AddEquality(key, value string, options ...string)` and `func (d *Distance) AddPriority(key, value string, patterns ...PatternOption)`, with the compile helpers returning `[]PatternOption`. The harness's mixed option decode adapts to the typed pair. It does not dictate the engine's signature. |
| internal/engine/matcher/unidecode | `internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/install.go:43` | high | InstallOptions.Geteuid is an injected function field. Its one production caller (cmd/dubplate-server/unidecode.go:69) always passes os.Geteuid, and only install_test.go:41 (foreign) ever passes anything else. A test seam is now part of the exported surface, and a zero InstallOptions panics at checkOwner (BACKLOG #177 already logs this). internal/store/leaf.go:63 solves the same problem the stdlib way, so the repo holds two patterns and this one is the wrong one to spread. auth.StoreRowOptions already copied it. | Drop the field and declare `var geteuid = os.Geteuid` at package level, as store/leaf.go does. checkOwner(info, path) then reads geteuid(), and the tests swap the var with t.Cleanup to restore it. InstallOptions keeps only Client. |
| internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/unidecodetest | `internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/unidecodetest/unidecodetest.go:15` | high | LoadCachedTable(tb testing.TB) is shaped for a caller that does not exist. A grep finds exactly three outside callers: internal/engine/matcher/testmain_test.go:18, internal/engine/tag/testmain_test.go:19 and internal/harness/conform/testmain_test.go:19. All three are TestMain functions, which have a *testing.M and no TB. So each one declares the same adapter, `type mainTB struct{ testing.TB }`, with Helper and a Fatalf that prints and calls os.Exit (matcher/testmain_test.go:26-33, copied verbatim in tag and conform). The embedded TB is nil. If LoadCachedTable ever calls tb.TempDir, tb.Cleanup or tb.Errorf, all three TestMains nil-panic instead of failing cleanly. The signature has already been copied three times, and an agent adding a fourth package that needs the table will copy it again. BACKLOG #66 tracks the three shim copies, but the root cause is this signature, not the copies. | Return an error, the way a loader whose callers sit outside a test function should: `func LoadCachedTable() (*unidecode.Table, error)`, wrapping each stage (`fmt.Errorf("read cached wheel at %s: %w; run make unidecode-wheel", path, err)`). Each TestMain then becomes `table, err := unidecodetest.LoadCachedTable(); if err != nil { fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err); os.Exit(1) }`, and the three mainTB types and their methods go away. If a *testing.T caller ever appears, it can call t.Fatal(err) itself. |
| internal/harness/oracle | `internal/harness/oracle/oracle.go:31` | high | Options.Shim is an exported field that only tests set: oracle_test.go and cmd/harness/freeze_test.go:78,106,137. The one production caller, cmd/harness/freeze.go:40, passes only Venv, and the type's own doc says the field is 'for tests'. It also makes Venv conditionally meaningful ('unused when Shim is set'), and that rule lives only in a trailing comment. Every future Options user inherits a knob production never turns. | Drop the field. Inside the package, keep the stdlib seam form, a package var the tests swap: `var shimCommand = func(ctx context.Context, venv string, args ...string) *exec.Cmd {...}`. For cmd/harness, give runFreeze its own seam, `var derive = oracle.Derive`, and let freeze_test swap it rather than reach through oracle's options. |
| internal/intake | `internal/intake/config.go:326` | high | WindowsFrom (326), LimitsFrom (350), FreeSpaceFloor (381), SweepInterval (424) and CheckWorkers (431) each have exactly one production caller, cmd/dubplate-server/intake.go:29-41 and serve.go:265. That caller passes each result straight back into intake.Config.Limits, Config.Windows, Config.SweepInterval, Config.CheckWorkers (intake.go:36-43) or TusConfig.Floor (tus.go:42). Intake already imports settings, so the binary reads intake's own settings through intake's own functions and hands the results back. The three function fields always get the same function in production, and only tests vary them (intake_test.go:112-113). An experienced reviewer would see five exports, three injected fields and two exported types (Limits, Windows) that exist only for a round trip. | Have Config carry the accessor and the bound: `Settings *settings.Accessor; Allowlist []string`. New builds the readers internally (`in.limits = limitsFrom(cfg.Settings, cfg.Allowlist)`), and Start reads the interval and the worker count itself. Unexport limitsFrom, windowsFrom, freeSpaceFloor, Limits and Windows, and drop TusConfig.Floor. Tests swap the unexported field on the built Intake, as they already do for subs.freeBytes. |
| internal/notify | `internal/notify/notify.go:60` | high | Mailer.tlsConfig and Mailer.forceImplicitTLS are struct fields whose only writers are tests (notify_test.go:411, 412, 433, 472, 605, 636). New never sets them, and their own comments (60-71, 12 lines) say they are test-only. Every production Mailer carries dead state, Send's TLS branch at 127 has a second condition that production never takes, and TestSend_ImplicitTLS465 never touches port 465. | Use the stdlib seam shape, package-level vars a test swaps: `var implicitTLSPort = 465` (the test sets it to the ephemeral port, which removes forceImplicitTLS and exercises the real port comparison) and `var tlsConfig *tls.Config // nil keeps go-mail's default` (the test sets clientTLSConfig). Mailer then holds only cfg. |
| internal/ops/admin | `internal/ops/admin/admin.go:50` | high | Config.Window is an injected function field whose only production caller (cmd/dubplate-server/serve.go:246) always fills it with this package's own export, admin.ReauthWindow(acc) (config.go:77). ReauthWindow exists only so cmd can hand the package back its own reader, and the field exists only so gate_test.go:191 can inject a broken reader and the fixture can pin a 2h window. An experienced reader sees two pieces of exported surface whose job is to serve a test. The repo has already copied the shape twice (local.JobRetention at internal/ops/local/config.go:36, intake.FreeSpaceFloor at internal/intake/config.go:381), so it is spreading. | Let Config carry the source and let New derive the reader: `Settings *settings.Accessor` in Config, and in New `s.window = auth.DurationSetting(cfg.Settings, reauthWindowSetting)`. requireFresh reads `s.window(ctx)`. The same-package test overwrites `f.seam.window` after New. Delete the exported ReauthWindow. Fix local and intake the same way so the pattern stops being the local example. |
| internal/ops/local | `internal/ops/local/local.go:43` | high | Config.Retention and BackupConfig's GuardFraction, SnapshotKeep, and ArchiveRetention are func fields. The one production caller (cmd/dubplate-server/backup.go:99-103) always fills them with the same closures over one *settings.Accessor, through local.JobRetention (config.go:36) and local.BackupConfigFrom (config.go:46, which calls backup.Readers). The package already imports settings and owns jobRetentionSetting, so the indirection exists only so tests can pass literals (helpers_test.go:34-37). It is the pattern the read exists to catch: an injected function field every production caller fills the same way. It also makes two exports whose only job is to build the seam. An agent will copy 'export a closure factory, take a func field' for every new setting. | Take the accessor itself: `type Config struct { Store *store.Handle; Settings *settings.Accessor; Backup BackupConfig; Logger *slog.Logger; Now func() time.Time }`, with BackupConfig holding only Enabled, Remote, and LibraryRoot. start reads `r.settings.Duration(ctx, jobRetentionSetting)`, and the backup binder reads the three backup values the same way. Delete JobRetention and BackupConfigFrom, or reduce the latter to the cfg-only part. Tests seed a settings row the way config_test.go:41 seedSettingRow already does. |
| internal/ops/opshttp | `internal/ops/opshttp/authorize.go:19` | high | The authorizer interface has one production implementation, contextAuthorizer{} (carrier.go:35), and NewMount always passes it along with identity.ChannelCookie (mount.go:54). In production, the mount puts the verified caller on the context at mount.go:77 and the same package reads it back one call later at carrier.go:38. The admittedKey, admitted, withAdmitted, contextAuthorizer, and authorizer interface pieces exist so the fixture (helpers_test.go:181-226) can inject fakeAuthorizer. newAuthorizingSeam's nil and channel checks (authorize.go:39-46) guard a parameter whose only legal value is ChannelCookie: identity declares two channels and the command line is refused. Only opshttp_test.go:617-621 ever passes another. The same split makes the mount rebuild a path ("/"+name, mount.go:78) and clone the request so that handler.ServeHTTP can strip the "/" again (opshttp.go:137). An agent will copy this pattern of interface plus context plus validated constant for every future mount. Record 0003 puts the bearer mount at 0.9, so building its hook now is early. | Pass the verified caller as a value. Make admission a pure function, `func admit(op string, actor ops.Actor, granted ops.Privilege) error`, that checks the channel against identity.ChannelCookie and checks the privilege. Give the routes the caller directly, `type route func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, actor ops.Actor, granted ops.Privilege)`, and have mount.ServeHTTP look up `m.routes[name]` after verify. Drive tests through NewMount with a fake Verifier, which is already the func-typed seam. Delete carrier.go, the authorizer interface, and the channel parameter; the bearer mount at 0.9 can add a channel field when it ships. |
| internal/webapp | `internal/webapp/doctor.go:18` | high | RowStatus takes `resolve func(*config.Config) error`, and its one production caller (cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:120) always passes webRowResolve(environ). The body holds no webapp knowledge: it checks for nil, calls the closure, and formats the result. Record 0013 places a health check with its fact, but here the fact lives in cmd (web.go:84-98) and only the string formatting lives here. ### P2. Exported surface ahead of a production caller

Record 0003 says nothing is exported ahead of a production caller, and a test is not a caller. These sites export a type, field, function, or constant that only tests or the declaring package use, or export more of a type than its callers read. `deadcode` does not catch most of them, because it skips variables, fields, and constants and treats anything reachable from main as used.

Fix: Unexport the identifier, or move it to a `_test.go` file or an `export_test.go` hook. Where a production caller is planned, land the export with that caller. The Unused exports section rules on each identifier.

83 sites in 33 scopes: 32 high, 39 medium, 12 low.

| Package | Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| internal/auth | `internal/auth/store.go:83` | high | Much of the exported surface has no caller outside the package. No code outside it calls Store.NoncePath or EpochPath (store.go:83,87), RecordID (store.go:108), EpochStore.Bump, CheckSession or CheckLink (epoch.go:101,225,251), Limiter.AllowConsumption or AllowLinkMail (limit.go:100,109), RefreshSession, SessionCookie or ClearSessionCookie (session.go:137,171,196), LinkToken (token.go:83), or SettingError (handlers.go:80). The same holds for twelve sentinels: ErrSessionRevoked, ErrLinkRevoked, ErrEpochBackward, ErrRateLimited, ErrNonceReplayed, the four ErrSession* (session.go:49-63) and the three ErrLink* (token.go:69-77). StoreDir, NonceLength and NonceStore.Insert are reached only by cmd tests. Their doc comments name in-package callers ('the session middleware', 'the consumption handler'), and gateRefusals (admin.go:76) is the only code that branches on the session sentinels. Record 0003 says nothing is exported ahead of a production caller. A wide surface tells the next agent that these are the package's API. | Unexport each one: noncePath, epochPath, recordID, storeDir, bump, checkSession, checkLink, allowConsumption, allowLinkMail, refreshSession, sessionCookie, clearSessionCookie, linkToken, settingError, and errSessionRevoked and the rest of the sentinels. Keep exported only what cmd, ops/admin and intake reference: Roster and its methods, the Err* the roster write path returns, ErrNotSignedIn, the key and setting declarations, and the constructors. |
| internal/backup | `internal/backup/doctor.go:28` | high | RemoteRowOptions is a one-field struct whose only field, FeaturesTimeout, is a timeout. Record 0014 says 'A timeout is a deadline on the request's context.' The export also brings the caller's UI into this package: RemoteRowStatus and RemoteRowOptions name doctor's table row, not what the function does. featuresStatus already decides 'not checked' from probeCtx.Err(), so a caller-supplied deadline gives the same behavior. | Delete the type. The caller sets the deadline: `ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(cmd.Context(), backupFeaturesTimeout); backup.CheckRemote(ctx, environ)`. The function becomes `func CheckRemote(ctx context.Context, environ []string) (status string, failed bool)`, and featuresStatus loses its timeout parameter. |
| internal/backup | `internal/backup/remote.go:21` | high | ParseRemote and the Remote type (remote.go:11) have no caller outside the package. Every reference is in backup.go:118, doctor.go:46, fetch.go:176, restore.go:156 and 375, or in same-package tests. Remote's fields and its only method, join, are unexported, so another package cannot do anything with a Remote value. Record 0003 says nothing is exported ahead of a production caller. Exports like this read as public API and invite agents to import them. | Unexport both: `func parseRemote(s string) (remote, error)`, with the type renamed so it does not collide with the many `remote` locals (for example `dest`). Change the field docs at backup.go:36 and restore.go:77 to say 'name:path, as rclone reads it' instead of linking [ParseRemote]. |
| internal/engine/matcher | `internal/engine/matcher/info.go:186` | high | ProjectItemFields decodes `map[string]any` shaped like a corpus case's JSON Input.Items, including float64-for-int tolerance. ### P3. Files split by deliverable, not by concept

Files follow the task or pass that produced them (one file per ledger task, one per doctor row family, one per verb) rather than one concept per file. Helpers then sit in the file of whichever deliverable needed them first, and a reader cannot guess where a function lives.

Fix: Split files by concept: one type and its methods, one wire format, one storage concern. Move shared helpers to the file of the concept they serve, and name the file for that concept.

33 sites in 28 scopes: 0 high, 16 medium, 17 low.

| Package | Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Doctor rows are spread over doctor.go (wall, threshold, and identity rows plus a 120-line RunE) and doctorrows.go (settings rows, backup rows, byte and age formatting). backupFeaturesTimeout, used only by doctor, sits at the bottom of backup.go:252. Route names are spread over serve.go (four const blocks), web.go, api.go:18, and intake.go:21. intake.go holds no intake verb, only serve wiring. newDoctorCmd's RunE is a gocognit exclusion (.golangci.yml:64), while backup and restore each lift their bodies into runX, so the package handles the same shape two ways. | Use routes.go for every route name, webRouteNames, and the Register calls; doctor.go for the verb, a runDoctor returning []doctorRow, printRow, and the row-name constants; doctorconfig.go for the wall, threshold, and identity-source statuses; and doctorstore.go for the settings and backup facts rows with their formatters and backupFeaturesTimeout. Fold api.go and intake.go into serve wiring (servewire.go). Lifting runDoctor out of the closure also takes newDoctorCmd off the exclusion list. |

Merge fetchunidecode.go and fetchxsd.go into fetch.go. main.go keeps only main and newRootCmd. |

### P4. Comment density and comments that do not state the rule

Comment density runs at two to five times the stdlib packages each read used as a reference. The excess is rarely doc comments on exported API. It is process citations (spec line numbers, rung and task numbers), rebuttals of review comments, defenses of a copy, and comments that no longer match the code. A false or stale comment costs more than none, because an agent trusts it.

Fix: State the invariant or the reason in the comment and delete the history, which belongs in commits and specs. Delete comments that restate the code or defend a duplicate (remove the duplicate instead). Correct the stale ones.

69 sites in 42 scopes: 0 high, 24 medium, 45 low.

| Package | Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Move each ordering reason in serve.go:95-119 to the line it governs as a one-line comment. Cut the nil-channel note to `// A nil channel never fires: each signal cancels once.` and cut each restating doc to one sentence on what the function returns. |

| web | `web/embed_test.go:63` | low | The "independent scanner so neither can share a blind spot" rationale appears four times: lines 63-64, 105-106, 202-204, and 222-225. It defends the absence of a shared helper that nobody proposed. | Say it once, at the top of the test file or on the first scanner, or drop it. |

Trim @component blocks to purpose, contract, and failure mode. Delete the paraphrase docs. |

### P5. Hand-kept parallel lists

One closed set (operation names, failure codes, method sets, case ids, file names) is written out in two to four places that must agree by hand, often with a comment asking the next editor to keep them in step.

Fix: Declare the set once, as a typed table or a const block, and derive the other forms from it (a map, a switch generated by range, a test that walks the table). Where a second form must exist, add a test that fails when the two disagree.

### P6. Helpers copied across packages

A helper, constant, or format is copied from one package into another, and a comment names the original instead of importing it.

Fix: Export the helper once from the package that owns the concept, or move it to a small leaf package, and delete the copies and the comments that point at the original.

| internal/harness/oracle | `internal/harness/oracle/oracle.go:40` | medium | The miss-file name is a contract between packages, and three hand-kept copies hold it. |
| internal/ops/opshttp | `internal/ops/opshttp/mount.go:16` | medium | opshttp.ErrUnauthenticated restates ops.ErrUnauthenticated (internal/ops/errors.go:44), which already maps to CodeUnauthenticated and which nothing in production raises today. |
| internal/harness/subsonic/fixture | `internal/harness/subsonic/fixture/manifest.go:25` | low | vorbisCommentBlock = 4 restates tagio's unexported blockVorbisComment (internal/engine/tagio/container.go:23). |

### P7. Error text: prefixes and stuttering wraps

Error messages follow no one convention. Some packages prefix every sentinel with the package name and others do not, some packages use another package's prefix, and some wraps repeat the context the callee already added.

Fix: Make sentinels bare lowercase phrases, let each `%w` wrap add only the context the callee lacks, and record the rule in the conventions doc.

8 sites in 7 scopes. The full finding for each is in its package table, marked P7.

| cmd/harness | `cmd/harness/recordoracle.go:315` | low | `at := fmt.Errorf(...)` builds a fresh error on every request just to serve as a prefix, and each use wraps it with `%w: %w`. |
| internal/atomicfile | `internal/atomicfile/atomicfile.go:67` | low | The error verbs disagree for the same operation. |
| internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/unidecodetest | `internal/engine/matcher/unidecode/unidecodetest/unidecodetest.go:22` | low | Messages written by package unidecodetest carry the prefix "unidecode:" (:22, :26, :30), and the test pins that prefix (unidecodetest_test.go:44). |
| internal/harness/oracle | `internal/harness/oracle/oracle.go:196` | low | The error message stutters. |

| internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule | `internal/harness/subsonic/xmlrule/schema.go:121` | low | Error prefixes are inconsistent. |

### P8. Closed sets typed as plain strings, and zero values that mean something

A closed set of values is a plain `string` or an untyped constant, so nothing ties a field to its legal values, or a type's zero value is a valid member, so a failed parse looks like a real answer.

Fix: Give each closed set a named type with its constants, validate at the boundary, and reserve the zero value for "unknown" (start `iota` at 1 or add an explicit Unknown).

6 sites in 6 scopes. The full finding for each is in its package table, marked P8.

| internal/config | `internal/config/thresholds.go:14` | medium | ThresholdKind is also the type of Setting.Kind (settings.go:63), so the name is wrong for half its uses. |
| internal/engine/tagio | `internal/engine/tagio/sniff.go:17` | medium | FormatFLAC is iota, so Format's zero value is FLAC. |
| internal/ops | `internal/ops/privilege.go:69` | medium | The twelve operation names live in parallel hand-kept lists: the untyped Op constants (admin.go:111-119, submissions.go:10-16), the privileges map (privilege.go:69-83), and opshttp's route table (opshttp/opshttp.go:98-110). |

### P9. Tests that restate each other or pin a count

Tests repeat another test's fixture and assertion, check a claim narrower than their name, or pin a count that every new entry must edit. They add edits without adding protection.

Fix: Delete the duplicate test, name each test for what it checks, and replace a pinned count with a property of the table (every entry has copy, every code is unique).

| internal/harness | `internal/harness/corpus_test.go:427` | medium | TestLoadCaseRejectsCorruptedFixture repeats cassette_test.go:258 TestLoadCaseCorruptedFixture: same fixture, same nil check, same "eval_date" substring. |
| internal/atomicfile | `internal/atomicfile/atomicfile_test.go:299` | low | The tests restate each other. |
| internal/harness/stub | `internal/harness/stub/stub_test.go:154` | low | Unused is a Server method, but its tests are named TestUnused* while every other test is TestServer*. |
| internal/opensubsonic | `internal/opensubsonic/boundary_test.go:57` | low | `TestLayerImportsNothingInternal` claims more than it checks. |
| internal/reject | `internal/reject/reject_test.go:99` | low | TestSetHoldsTwentySixBlockingAndThreeWarnings is a change detector that every new reason must edit. |

## By package

Each scope gives its verdict, then the findings that patterns P1 to P4 do not hold, high severity first. A finding marked P5 to P9 is also listed under that pattern.

Note: Two web reads (accessibility and structure) proposed exporting a module-level `siteName` constant from `web/src/lib/copy/title.ts` to replace the `browser` guards. The Svelte read says that cannot work, and the code agrees: `web/scripts/rendered/run.ts:70` imports `title.ts` under Node, where `document` does not exist. That fix variant is dropped, and the finding stays with the lazy-helper fix.

### cmd/dubplate-server

Proud: no. Verdict: Workmanlike. This is the binary's composition root: cobra verbs, the serve shell's wiring, and the doctor table. Its runtime judgment is careful: signal handling, exit statuses that never depend on error text, secret-safe doctor rows. What costs it most is test-only seams built into production signatures, which agents will copy: operatorVerbs, the mount func on newServeShellVia, the FromFS twin, authHandlersConfig, and stdout/stderr threaded into every verb. Comment density is DENSITY: 0.297 (516/1740) against cmd/go/internal/modcmd at 0.141 (241/1704), about twice the stdlib, and most of the excess justifies those same seams.

13 findings: 3 high, 7 medium, 3 low. Held by patterns: P1 (4), P3 (1), P4 (1).

| Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/backup.go:63` | medium | runBackup repeats openOperatorStore's body line for line (loadConfig, store.BusyTimeout, store.OpenWrite), and runRestore (restore.go:145-152) repeats its first two steps. member list (member.go:201-214) rebuilds withStore by hand and then calls List on the unbounded cmd.Context(), not the timed one, so the one verb that skipped the helper also lost its bound. `slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(stderr, nil))` appears four times (backup.go:72, member.go:125, member.go:181, restore.go:182). | Have openOperatorStore return `(*store.Handle, *config.Config, error)` and use it in backup and member list. Build the verb logger once, for example `func verbLogger(w io.Writer) *slog.Logger`, or in a root PersistentPreRunE. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:185` | medium | The doctor row-name constants follow no single scheme. clientIPDoctorRow, webDoctorRow, unidecodeDoctorRow, musicVolumeDoctorRow, authStoreDoctorRow, and membersDoctorRow use one suffix; backupRemoteRow and backupLastSuccessRow..backupLocalCopiesRow (doctor.go:198-204) use another; adminEmailKey is a third form; the 'store' row is a bare literal (doctor.go:100). They sit in six separate const blocks, each with its own one-line comment. doctorRow is also the struct type, so `xDoctorRow` reads as a value of that type. | Use one block, `// Doctor's fixed row keys. const ( rowStore = "store"; rowMembers = "members"; rowAuthStore = "auth store"; rowBackupRemote = ...; rowClientIP = ... )`, with one prefix for all and no per-constant comment that repeats the string. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/doctor.go:283` | medium | thresholdStatus repeats config's kind table (ParseInt/ParseDuration/ParseFloat/ParseString per kind) in a switch whose default arm reports 'a registration bug'. config already owns that dispatch in ThresholdKind.Parse (internal/config/thresholds.go:48), and every formatted value here (strconv.Itoa, d.String, FormatFloat 'g' -1) is what fmt.Sprint gives for the same types. A new kind would have to be added in two packages. | Have config export one resolver, such as `config.Resolve(environ, t) (any, error)` (the any-typed form of parseThreshold). The doctor arm becomes `v, err := config.Resolve(environ, t); ...; return fmt.Sprint(v)`. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/web.go:71` | medium | newWebRoutes and newWebRoutesFromFS are twins that differ only in calling embeddedBuild. serve skips newWebRoutes and calls embeddedBuild itself (serve.go:121), then newWebRoutesFromFS (serve.go:272), so only doctor's webRowResolve calls newWebRoutes. The route table is also kept by hand in three places: the constants (serve.go:47-55, web.go:19-24), the webRouteNames struct (web.go:28), and registerWebRoutes plus the two admin-gated registrations back in serve.go:277-278. A new route has to be added to all three. | Keep one function, `newWebRoutes(environ, prefix, siteName, stateDir string, logger, build fs.FS)`, and have both callers pass embeddedBuild's result. Put the names, webRouteNames, and every Register call for web routes, admin-gated ones included, in one routes.go. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/backup.go:239` | low | backupExitCode returns an error, not a code. exitCodeErr (main.go:82) breaks the Go convention that error types end in Error (exec.ExitError, fs.PathError). In awaitBackup, requestCancel, and reportOutcome's docs, `h` means an ops.Handle (a job id, internal/ops/ops.go:99), when everywhere else in the package `h` is *store.Handle. reportOutcome's doc talks about 'h' but its parameter is `job`. | Rename exitCodeErr to exitError (variable `ee`) and backupExitCode to backupExitError or withExitStatus. Rename the job parameter to `jobID` and fix reportOutcome's doc to name `job`. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/intake.go:73` | low | tusVerifier and apiVerifier (api.go:32) share the same CheckRequest call and ErrNotSignedIn switch, with a different sentinel wrapped. The DUBPLATE_STATE_DIR literal appears at doctor.go:114 and unidecode.go:65. adminEmailKey (doctor.go:183) copies internal/auth/doctor.go:21 verbatim. | Add `func checkSession(cfg auth.AdminConfig, w, r, unauth error) (auth.Verified, error)` and call it from both verifiers. Use exported key constants from config instead of copied literals. |
| `cmd/dubplate-server/web.go:45` | low | privateFiles wraps one string, and every user unpacks it straight away (`webapp.FromEnviron(environ, private.stateDir)`, web.go:115). Tests always pass the zero value.
