Since 5.16, Svelte takes arrays and objects in `class`. | `class={['input validator', erroredField === 'address' && 'input-error']}` (svelte.dev/docs/svelte/class). |
| `web/src/routes/members/+page.svelte:386` | low | At 386 lines the page holds the list rows, the add-member form, and the confirm modal. The confirm dialog (369-385, with `ask`, `closed` and `confirm` at 220-263) is a self-contained unit, and the `'remove' \| 'demote'` literal is written out three times (109, 220, 233). | Extract a `ConfirmDialog.svelte` with a snippet or a callback prop, define `type MemberChange = 'remove' \| 'demote'` once, and consider a `MemberRow.svelte`. |
| `web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte:91` | low | `editable` and `environmental` repeat the same predicate, once negated, so the two lists can drift. The loop variable `e` (91-93, 104-111, 231-237, 252, 300) conventionally means an event or error; here it holds a setting. | Write `const isEditable = (s: Setting) => s.home === 'store' && s.source !== 'environment';` and derive both lists from it, or use one `$derived.by` that partitions. Rename `e` to `setting`. |

### web:a11y

Proud: no. Verdict: The styling and accessibility work is unusually careful: measured inks, one focus rule with halos, a native modal dialog with focus return, and always-present live regions. The page code below the components is not at that level. The two admin pages copy one state machine under different names, breaking the repo's own W10.4 rule. lib/api hands DaisyUI class names and page states to its callers. And a session that ends partway through a write drops focus with no announcement.

31 findings: 3 high, 12 medium, 16 low.

| Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `web/src/lib/api/outcome.ts:39` | high | The transport layer chooses DaisyUI classes and UI states. outcomeAlertClass (outcome.ts:39) and failureAlertClass (v1.ts:110) return 'alert-warning' or 'alert-error'. loadList (v1.ts:73) returns page states ('signedOut', 'unreachable') and copy-deck text. Those class strings then travel through page state (Notice.alertClass) into markup as `class="alert {notice.alertClass}"`. outcome.ts already has the right abstraction, OutcomeClass ('status' \| 'warning' \| 'error'), but keeps it internal and exports CSS instead. Swapping the styling layer, or adding a third tone, would mean editing lib/api. | Have lib/api return data only: a result kind and a Tone union, with OutcomeClass renamed to Tone and exported because it would then have callers. Map Tone to the alert-* class in one view place, such as a WriteNotice prop `tone: Tone`. Move outcomeText and failureText out of lib/api into lib/copy. |
| `web/src/routes/members/+page.svelte:77` | high | The admin-page state machine is copied into both admin pages, and the copies have drifted apart. Members has Notice (line 77), say(text, alertClass, politeness, reauthenticate) (148), settle(result, again): boolean (159), submitting, loadRoster and refreshAfterWrite. Server settings has a different Notice with an extra key (67), say(n: Notice) (149), refuse(result, key, again): void (155), busy, load and refreshAfterWrite(key). WriteNotice.svelte:13 declares a third inline Notice shape. Both pages also render the same refreshFailed alert (members:309, settings:246). The repo's own W10.4 rule says a shape copied into a second file is extracted to one home. The next admin page will copy whichever version it sees first. | Extract one create* closure (W10.1 forbids classes), for example createAdminList<T>(operation, field) in #lib/admin.svelte.ts. It returns rune-backed status, entries, refreshFailed, notice, busy, and load, refresh, report(result, again) and succeed(text). Export one Notice type from WriteNotice's module script. Let AdminPage render the refresh-failed alert. |
| `web/src/routes/members/+page.svelte:161` | high | When a write comes back 'unauthenticated', settle() sets status = 'signedOut' and returns false. addMember then awaits focusRefusal() (line 193), which looks up OUTCOME_TEXT_ID or a field id. AdminPage has already unmounted both, so focus falls to <body> and nothing is announced. Server settings does the same at line 156: refuse() sets status = 'signedOut' and returns without announcing. A screen-reader user who presses Add member or Save hears nothing, while sighted users see the page change. This fails WCAG 2.4.3 (Focus Order) and 4.1.3 (Status Messages), and every new admin page inherits it. | Have AdminPage own state transitions. When status changes to 'signedOut', 'refused' or 'unreachable' after mount, give its h1 tabindex="-1" and focus it in a $effect, or announce API_REFUSALS.unauthenticated through announce(..., 'assertive'). Pages then stop focusing ids that no longer exist. |
| `web/src/lib/components/AdminPage.svelte:54` | medium | The page heading is split between AdminPage and its children. AdminPage renders `<h1>{title}</h1>` for the unreachable state from its `title` prop, and each child page renders the same h1 from the same constant (members:267, server-settings:242). The class string `display page-title text-4xl` appears in 7 places. A new admin page has to remember to repeat both the heading and its classes. | Have AdminPage render the h1 from `title` in the unreachable and ok states, with children starting below it. Bake the size into `.page-title` in components.css, or make a <PageTitle> component, so the recipe has one home. |
| `web/src/lib/components/SiteHeader.svelte:29` | medium | The header component holds two domain modules. The first is the sign-out POST: the endpoint name SIGN_OUT_PATH, the call, and the rule about which synthetic outcomes to trust (lines 30, 78-97). The other two auth endpoints already live in #lib/api/auth.ts as requestLink and consumeLink. The second is theme persistence: the cookie name and format, the theme stamp names and the theme-color meta rewrite (lines 29-72). This duplicates knowledge the Go side and system.css also hold. A future settings page with a theme picker would copy it again. | Add `signOut(): Promise<AuthResult>` to #lib/api/auth.ts, with the trust rule inside it. Move the THEME_COOKIE, stamp names, currentlyDark, the cookie write and restampThemeColorMeta into #lib/theme.ts, exposing for example setPalette(palette) and isDark(). SiteHeader then only wires events. |
| `web/src/lib/components/SiteHeader.svelte:114` | medium | The repo's W10.6 rule says icons are one module of SVG path data keyed by name. SiteHeader instead inlines two full <svg> elements, each with a path string of several hundred characters, in the template. The pattern the next icon will copy is pasted SVG markup in a component. | Create #lib/icons.ts with `export const ICONS = { sun: '...', moon: '...' }` and a use comment for each, plus a small <Icon name="sun" /> that renders `<svg aria-hidden="true" viewBox="0 0 256 256" fill="currentColor"><path d={ICONS[name]} /></svg>`. Keep PHOSPHOR.md beside icons.ts. |
| `web/src/lib/components/SiteHeader.svelte:148` | medium | A scoped <style> media block (max-width: 479px) silently overrides the utilities in the markup. The markup says gap-4 and text-2xl, but a phone gets 0.5rem and 1.25rem because the scoped selectors outrank them. It also reaches into Mark's internals with `:global(img)` and sets width and height to 24px, contradicting the width={size} that Mark used to pick its asset. The comment's premise, 'a breakpoint cannot read a custom property', is wrong for Tailwind 4: `@theme { --breakpoint-xs: 30rem; }` generates xs: and max-xs: variants. A reader of the markup cannot see what actually renders. | Declare `--breakpoint-xs: 30rem` in an @theme block in app.css and write the responsive values in markup: `gap-2 xs:gap-4`, `text-xl xs:text-2xl`, `px-2 xs:px-4` on the ghost button. Drive the mark's size from a prop, or put `class="size-6 xs:size-8"` on Mark, instead of a :global override. |
| `web/src/lib/components/WriteNotice.svelte:13` | medium | The props can represent an invalid state. `notice.reauthenticate: true` can arrive with `retry: null`, and the Try again button then renders and does nothing (`retry?.()`, line 36). The pairing is kept only by convention in each page (`if (reauthenticate) retry = again`), and both pages hold `retry` as a separate $state beside `notice`. | Fold the retry into the notice, for example `reauthenticate?: { retry: () => Promise<void> }`, so the sign-in link and retry render exactly when a retry exists. Delete the separate retry state in both pages. |
| `web/src/lib/components/WriteNotice.svelte:36` | medium | Several copy constants are named for one place and used in others. ROSTER_RETRY is the retry label on the server settings page (WriteNotice:36, AdminPage:56). ADD_MEMBER_LABELS.address labels the login page's email field (login/+page.svelte:124). ERROR_PAGE.notFoundHeading is rendered as body text in a <p>, while the real heading is TITLES.notFound (+error.svelte:23, AdminPage.svelte:48). TITLES.error and ERROR_PAGE.genericHeading are near-duplicate strings that differ only by a final period (deck.ts:310 and 331). The names describe where a string first appeared rather than what it is. | Rename by role: RETRY_CONTROL, EMAIL_ADDRESS_LABEL (shared by login and add-member), ERROR_PAGE.notFoundBody. Drop ERROR_PAGE.genericHeading and use TITLES.error. |
| `web/src/lib/themes/dubplate.css:26` | medium | The `daisyui/theme` blocks leave `themecontroller` at its default, so every composed theme is emitted with the `:root:has(input.theme-controller[value=...]:checked)` branch at specificity (0,4,1). The built bundle carries it for dubplate-label and dubplate-label-night. DL R4.6 and the long specificity argument in system.css:13-20 exist to reason around that branch. daisyUI 5.8.1's theme plugin offers `themecontroller: false` (theme/index.js), which emits `[data-theme="x"]` alone. This is separate from the recorded W5.9 ruling on prefersdark and the built-in themes. | Add `themecontroller: false;` to the four dubplate-label and dubplate-label-night blocks (dubplate.css, label.css, label-night.css). The hazard then cannot occur, and the specificity caveat in system.css can shrink to one line. Have G2 assert the option. |
| `web/src/routes/+error.svelte:17` | medium | `browser ? siteNameFromDocument(document) : ''` is repeated in +error.svelte:17, AdminPage.svelte:36, login/+page.svelte:27 and login/link/+page.svelte:176-177. The guard is dead code: the root +layout.ts sets `ssr = false`, so components never run on the server, and kit's client build of $app/env exports `browser = true`. Each copy teaches the next page to add a guard and to re-read the meta tag. | Remove the guards. Add one `servedSiteName()` helper to title.ts that reads `document` lazily, or a `pageTitle(state)` that calls it, and import that wherever a title is built. (The read also proposed a module-level `siteName` constant; that variant is dropped. See the note at the start of By package.) |
| `web/src/routes/members/+page.svelte:325` | medium | The add-member inputs (lines 325, 339 and 357) carry the DaisyUI `validator` class, so a field turns its border red on :user-invalid after blur. No validator-hint and no aria-invalid accompany it until a server round trip, so the error is shown by color alone until the browser bubble appears on submit. The login page has a validator-hint (line 138), but its `hidden` attribute (preflight's display:none !important) defeats DaisyUI's own show-on-invalid rule, so the hint appears only for a server refusal. This fails WCAG 1.4.1 (Use of Color) and 3.3.1 (Error Identification). | Use DaisyUI's validator-hint pattern as designed: put a `<p class="validator-hint hidden">` (the class, not the attribute) after each validator input, so DaisyUI's `display: revert-layer` shows it on :user-invalid, and tie it with aria-describedby. Or drop `validator` from fields that have no client-side hint. |
| `web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte:48` | medium | The wire types for settings are declared inside the page (Entry at 48, Written at 60), while Member lives in #lib/api/v1.ts. Every enumerated field is typed as `string` (home, apply, mark, source) and compared against literals ('store', 'environment', 'restart', 'admin', 'default'), so a server rename fails silently. Written declares key and source, but only apply is read. | Move Setting and SettingWritten into #lib/api/v1.ts beside Member, with literal unions taken from openapi.yaml, for example `home: 'store' \| 'environment'` and `apply: 'immediately' \| 'next_operation' \| 'restart'`. Type SETTING_APPLY and SETTING_SOURCES as Record over those unions, and drop the unused fields. |
| `web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte:177` | medium | The line `void tick().then(() => ...focus())` breaks the repo's W10.2 rule, async/await only, whose `.then` half the standard marks as ESLint-gated. web/ has no ESLint config or dependency, so the claimed gate does not exist, and W10.1's no-classes gate is also unenforced. Members does the same job with `await focusRefusal()`, so the two pages are inconsistent. | Make refuse() async and `await tick()` before focusing, matching members' focusRefusal. Separately, either add the ESLint gate W10.1 and W10.2 cite or relabel those clauses as review-held. |
| `web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte:256` | medium | Each settings input is labelled only by the raw key name (font-mono text-xs, 12px). The plain-language description (line 259) and the source and apply line (line 260) are not programmatically associated with the field. aria-describedby is set only in the error state (line 280). A screen-reader user tabbing through fields hears names like `foo_bar_baz` with no description, which fails WCAG 1.3.1 (Info and Relationships) and 3.3.2 (Labels or Instructions). | Give the description and apply <p> elements ids derived from fieldId(e.name), for example `${fieldId(e.name)}-desc`. Set `aria-describedby` to them always, and append 'settings-outcome' when the notice names this key. |
| `web/src/lib/announcer.svelte.ts:27` | low | I am uncertain about this one. The announcer clears its region and rewrites it one animation frame later (about 16ms). Some screen-reader and browser pairs coalesce changes that close together, so a repeated identical message can be dropped. The regions in +layout.svelte:25-26 also lack aria-atomic. | Rewrite after a short timeout (about 100ms) instead of rAF, and add `aria-atomic="true"` to both regions. Verify with NVDA and VoiceOver. |
| `web/src/lib/api/outcome.ts:10` | low | `export type OutcomeClass` has no importer. Its only other appearance is a local redeclaration in scripts/rendered/run.ts:2525. Record 0003 says nothing is exported ahead of a production caller. | Unexport it, or give it a production caller by adopting the Tone change in the lib/api boundary finding. |
| `web/src/lib/api/post.ts:28` | low | endpointPath, the base-path resolver, lives in the form-POST module, and v1.ts imports post.ts just to get it. The URL concern belongs with request.ts, which the module header calls the one place the app calls fetch. | Move baseRoot and endpointPath into request.ts (or a paths.ts) and have post.ts and v1.ts import them from there. |
| `web/src/lib/components/AdminPage.svelte:57` | low | The `loading` state renders an empty <main> with no busy signal and no text. | `web/src/lib/components/SiteHeader.svelte:46` | low | `dark` is computed once at mount. On the dubplate-system theme stamp, an OS light/dark change leaves the checkbox showing the old state until reload. | Subscribe to matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)') change events while the system theme stamp is active, using a $state boolean, or document the limitation. |
| `web/src/lib/components/WriteNotice.svelte:35` | low | The 'Sign in again' link opens a new tab (target=_blank) without telling the user. | `web/src/lib/styles/components.css:437` | low | Home-grown recipe classes (.display, .page-title, .content-measure, .flow) are plain selectors in `@layer utilities`, mixed in with the DaisyUI overrides. Several comments rest on references a reader cannot follow, such as SiteHeader.svelte:50 '(R-5ciii9)' and components.css:271 'Ruling 7; the db1e111 lesson'. | Re-verify and re-pin the version notes, update deck.ts's header, and state the reason inline wherever a comment currently just cites an ID. |

I am uncertain here: the match-axis model may intend fonts to vary per composed theme in future. | If fonts are not meant to vary per theme, declare `@theme { --font-sans: 'Work Sans', ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; ... }` once in app.css and drop them from the theme blocks, system.css and G2's parity rows. |

### web:structure

Proud: no. Verdict: The small modules are well made: link-token, announcer, title, and the exhaustive ApiResult switch. Their comments carry a real reason or constraint. lib/api is not one client, though. It has two transports with three result shapes, and it returns deck lines, DaisyUI classes, and page-state names, so the boundary runs the wrong way. The two admin pages each build the same onMount status machine and write cycle by hand, with names that have drifted apart, where SvelteKit's load, error, and invalidate do this work. That is the pattern the next admin page will copy.

17 findings: 5 high, 6 medium, 6 low. Held by patterns: P4 (2).

| Site | Severity | Finding | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `web/src/lib/api/post.ts:42` | high | lib/api is two clients, not one. post.ts returns `PostResult { ok, outcome: string, body }`, which models failures as synthetic strings (`'network_failure'`, `'cross_origin'`, `'failed'`). v1.ts:27 returns a discriminated `ApiResult<T>` for the same failure kinds. v1.ts:70-73 adds a third shape, `{ ok: true; entries } \| { ok: false; state; message }`. The two clients also read the same server defense differently: post.ts:62 treats any 403 as cross_origin before parsing, while v1.ts:55-56 does so only after the JSON parse fails. `endpointPath`, the shared URL builder, lives in post.ts and v1.ts:8 imports it from there. request.ts:7 wraps only `fetch`, so both clients re-implement the POST, the headers, the JSON parse, and the 403 reading. An experienced developer would object to one server with two error models: every new endpoint has to pick one, and pages must learn both. | Make request.ts the client. It should own the base root and `endpointPath`, one `post(name, body, encoding)`, and one `Result<T>` union (`ok \| refused{status,code,detail} \| cross_origin \| network_failure \| failed`). auth.ts and v1.ts then become thin per-endpoint functions over that union. The auth outcome becomes the `ok` value's typed field, not a string that synthetic failures share. |
| `web/src/lib/api/v1.ts:7` | high | The API layer imports the copy deck and emits presentation. v1.ts:85-113 (`failureText`, `failureAlertClass` returning `'alert-warning'` and `'alert-error'`) and v1.ts:77 and 82 (`loadList` returning a deck `message`) put UI strings and DaisyUI class names into the transport. outcome.ts as a whole (outcome-to-text, outcome-to-DaisyUI-class, politeness) is presentation filed under api/ (outcome.ts:7-8, 38-42). The two resolvers also apply the same class rule twice in different shapes: outcome.ts:19-28 and v1.ts:106-113. The dependency runs api -> copy -> UI, so changing a CSS framework or a sentence touches the client. | Keep lib/api free of deck and DaisyUI imports and have it return typed results only. Move outcome.ts and the failure* and needsReauthentication helpers into one presentation module, for example `lib/feedback.ts`, that maps either result kind to `{ text, tone, politeness }`. Components map `tone` to a DaisyUI class at the markup. |
| `web/src/lib/api/v1.ts:44` | high | (P8) The wire contract is typed as `string` throughout, even though `internal/ops/opshttp/openapi.yaml` declares enums for these fields (`home: [store, environment]` at :415, `apply` at :419, `mark` at :422, `source: [admin, env-seed, environment, default]` at :426). `call<T>(operation: string, ...)` is a return-only generic: `body as T` (:59) and `list as T[]` (:78) are unchecked assertions presented as type parameters. Wire types are defined in two different places: `Member` in v1.ts:12, but `Entry` and `Written` inside the page (server-settings:48-64), and `Entry.seed_value` (:56) is never read. Auth outcomes are `outcome: string` (auth.ts:19, post.ts:44), so pages compare raw literals (`'sent'`, `'requested'`, `'invalid_address'`, `'signed_in'`, `'refused'` at login/+page.svelte:50, 69, 77 and login/link/+page.svelte:51). A typo there compiles. | Generate types from openapi.yaml (openapi-typescript), or hand-write one `Operations` map keyed by operation name with request and response types, and type `call<K extends keyof Operations>(op: K, req: Operations[K]['req'])`. Declare `type AuthOutcome = 'sent' \| 'requested' \| ...` beside auth.ts. Remove `seed_value`. |
| `web/src/routes/members/+page.svelte:117` | high | The pages load their data in `onMount` (members:144-146, server-settings:145-147) and run a hand-built `Status` machine (`'loading' \| 'ok' \| 'refused' \| 'signedOut' \| 'unreachable'`, AdminPage.svelte:12). AdminPage.svelte:40-59 renders the gate states itself, and the not-found state duplicates +error.svelte's sentence (+error.svelte:20-23 says so). After a write, each page reloads its list with its own `refreshAfterWrite`. `loadList` in v1.ts:73 spells the page-state union a second time, independently of AdminPage's `Status`, so the API client knows a component's state names. SvelteKit already provides this: with ssr=false, a universal `+page.ts` load runs in the browser, `error(404)` renders +error.svelte, and `depends('api:members')` with `invalidate` reruns the load. The signed-in header is also rendered inside each page through AdminPage.svelte:43, not by a route-group layout. | Move the admin routes under `routes/(admin)/` with a `+layout.svelte` that holds SiteHeader and the `<main id="main">` shell. Give each page a `+page.ts` load that calls the client: it throws `error(404)` for forbidden, returns or redirects for signed-out, and throws for unreachable so +error.svelte can offer a retry through `invalidateAll`. Replace `refreshAfterWrite` with `invalidate('api:members')`. Delete AdminPage's gate renderer and the duplicated Status union. |
| `web/src/routes/server-settings/+page.svelte:149` | high | The members and settings pages each build the same admin-write cycle by hand, and the names have drifted. The in-flight flag is `submitting` (members:99) in one and `busy` (settings:88) in the other. The failure reader is `settle(result, again): boolean` (members:159) in one and `refuse(result, key, again): void` (settings:155) in the other. `say(text, alertClass, politeness, reauthenticate)` (members:148) and `say(n: Notice)` (settings:149) share a name but not a signature. Each page declares its own `Notice` interface (members:77, settings:67), and both differ from WriteNotice's inline prop type (WriteNotice.svelte:13). The list loaders are `loadRoster` in one and `load` in the other. The reset preamble (`notice = null; retry = null; announce('', 'polite')`) is repeated at members:185-188 and 236-239 and at settings:188-190. dupl misses this because the copies differ token by token. An agent writing the third admin page will copy whichever page it opens first. | Extract one rune class in a `.svelte.ts` module, for example `lib/admin-write.svelte.ts` exporting `class AdminWrite { busy; notice; retry; run(op, request, onOk) }`, that owns the in-flight guard, the reauthentication retry, the signed-out escalation, and the announcement. WriteNotice exports its `Notice` type, and both pages pass in only their own field-focus rule. |
| `web/package.json:8` | medium | The web app has no ESLint configuration: no eslint.config.* exists, and the only script is `svelte-check` (:11). The ts-conventions skill requires ESLint with the jsdoc and tsdoc plugins, so nothing mechanically holds comment shape or TSDoc syntax. ### repo:naming

Proud: no. Verdict: Each package is carefully written on its own: the comments are precise, the sentinels are mapped, and the id parser is a good model. But the core vocabulary was coined again at each package boundary. "Who acted", "not signed in", "seam", "setting reader", "id", and "setting key" each exist in two to four forms, joined by conversion code in cmd and internal/ops/admin. An agent copying any one package would add a fifth form instead of reusing one, so the boundary findings are high severity even though each package passes the gate.

Its 21 findings are merged under Repo-wide.

### repo:structure

Proud: no. Verdict: Locally the packages are careful. config is a true leaf, the ops/opshttp projection is clean, table ownership is tested, and composition sits in cmd. The boundaries between packages, though, have drifted in ways an agent would copy. Concepts are duplicated across packages (Actor, the member count, free-space probes, setting readers). Health checks and other packages' facts sit in cmd and store, against records 0013 and 0011. A process-wide transliterator global lives in the matcher. The harness tree is split by history rather than by job, and its on-disk case layout is hand-copied into cmd.

Its 20 findings are merged under Repo-wide.

## Unused exports

The reads found 241 exported identifiers with no production caller. Each has one ruling:

- keep-for-caller: 159. A production caller is planned or the identifier is part of a contract a planned caller needs, so it stays exported until that caller lands.
- move-to-test: 79. Only tests use it, so it moves to a `_test.go` file or an `export_test.go` hook.
- delete: 3. Nothing uses it and nothing will.

## Best passages

These three passages show the judgment the bar asks for. Each makes a careless caller or an unlucky failure safe, at the one point where the danger enters, with the reason stated in a sentence. Copy the habit, not only the code.

### A transaction that cannot autocommit by accident

`internal/store/write.go:104` (internal/store). Tx records its first statement error and refuses every later statement without reaching SQLite. The reason is that a transaction SQLite rolled back on its own leaves the connection in autocommit, so a callback that swallows an error would otherwise commit its remaining statements one at a time. This wrapper exists to make a careless caller safe, which is a judgment a linter cannot supply.

### A fault attributed from the gate's own reads

`internal/intake/gate/file.go:391` (internal/intake/gate). recorder.fault decides whose fault a decode failure is from the gate's own recorded reads, not from the decoder's error, which mewkiz/flac returns raw. The decode doc states the rule: any non-EOF read failure is the server's whatever the decoder said. That keeps a disk fault from becoming a verdict that deletes a member's bytes, a guarantee a linter cannot see.

### Cancellation checked before the exit status

`internal/backup/rclone.go:143` (internal/backup). classify checks ctx.Err() before the exit status because Wait reports the child's own exit after a cancel and rclone exits 130 on SIGINT. Ordering the checks that way is what keeps an operator's interrupt from being recorded as 'rclone-exit-130'. It comes from knowing how os/exec and rclone behave, not from a rule.

The reads named 158 strong passages in all. Close seconds are the strict base64 credential encoding in `internal/auth/token.go:245`, the fixed-point reparse in `internal/identity/identity.go:68`, and the shutdown on the failure path in `internal/serve/server.go:172`.
